Type this-is-provided-keyPrefix to confirm deletion: \n```\n\n**When** I mistype the autolink keyPrefix\n**Then** I see an informative error and prompted to try again to enter the autolink keyPrefix\n\n```shell\nX Sorry, your reply was invalid: You entered lkjalskdjfasdf\n? Type this-is-provided-keyPrefix to confirm deletion: \n```\n\n**When** I correctly type the autolink keyPrefix\n**Then** I see an informational message confirming the repository autolink was deleted\n\n```shell\n✓ Autolink \"<keyPrefix>\" deleted from <owner>/<repo>\n```\n\n---\n\n**Given** I have the `admin` role on the repository\n**And Given** I have a local repository cloned from GitHub\n**When** I run `gh repo autolink delete <keyPrefix>` in non-interactive mode\n**Then** I see an informational message stating `--yes` flag is required to delete autolink in non-interactive mode followed by the usage statement\n\n```shell\n--yes required when not running interactively\n\nUsage:  ...\n```\n\n> [!NOTE]\n> For examples, see [`gh label delete` behavior](https://github.com/cli/cli/blob/5402e207ee89f2f3dc52779c3edde632485074cd/pkg/cmd/label/delete.go#L44-L46)\n\n---\n\n**Given** I have the `admin` role on the repository\n**And Given** I have a local repository cloned from GitHub\n**When** I run `gh repo autolink delete <keyPrefix>` in interactive mode\n**Then** I see a prompt asking for confirmation of deleting autolink by typing keyPrefix\n\n---\n\n**Given** I don't have the `admin` role on the repository\n**And Given** I have a local repository cloned from GitHub\n**When** I run `gh repo autolink delete <keyPrefix>`\n**Then** I see an informative error after confirming prompt\n\n```shell\n? Describe the bug\n\nThe output of `gh attestation verify` with the `--format json` flag produces a result structure with an incorrectly-formatted in-toto attestation.\n\nFor example, the field `predicateType` is called `predicate_type` in the output, which isn't correct [according to the spec.](https://github.com/in-toto/attestation/blob/main/spec/v1/predicate.md)\n\nThis can be observed using this command:\n\n```shell\ngh attestation verify oci://ghcr.io/github/artifact-attestations-helm-charts/trust-policies:v0.6.2 --owner github --format json --jq .[0].verificationResult.statement\n```\n\ngh version:\n\n```\n ▶ gh --version\ngh version 2.59.0 (2024-10-15)\nhttps://github.com/cli/cli/releases/tag/v2.59.0\n```\n\nThe root cause is a problem with JSON encoding described in this issue: https://github.com/in-toto/attestation/issues/363\n\nRelated issue in sigstore-go: https://github.com/sigstore/sigstore-go/issues/365\n\nThis should be fixed by https://github.com/sigstore/sigstore-go/pull/366. After it is merged, a release will be cut, and `gh` may update to that version of sigstore-go.\n\n### Steps to reproduce the behavior\n\n1. Type this '...'\n2. View the output '....'\n3. command"}],"state":"open","locked":false,"assignee":null,"assignees":[],"milestone":null,"comments":7,"created_at":"2024-12-20T11:06:15Z","updated_at":"2025-03-27T03:44:07Z","closed_at":null,"author_association":"MEMBER","type":null,"active_lock_reason":null,"sub_issues_summary":{"total":0,"completed":0,"percent_completed":0},"issue_dependencies_summary":{"blocked_by":0,"total_blocked_by":0,"blocking":0,"total_blocking":0},"body":"## Description\n\nOriginally, the GitHub CLI only supported writing the token during obtained `auth login`, insecurely to a file. In fact it was only February 2023 that `gh` [began using keyrings for secure storage](https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/7043). When this was added, it was opt-in via `--secure-storage` because the team didn't want to break existing workflows. Later, this was [made the default](https://github.com/cli/cli/pull/7276) and the previous insecure behaviour was opt in via `--insecure-storage`. In order to avoid breaking anyone who didn't have a system keyring set up correctly, `gh` would fallback to the insecure storage if the keyring was unavailable.\n\nWe've had so many issues of people complaining about `gh` falling back to insecure storage, not being particularly transparent about doing that, and `gh` giving no indication of why the secure storage didn't work (because the error is swallowed) that I think we should do something about it:\n * https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/8954\n * https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/5961\n\nIt would be nice to have telemetry on:\n * How many / what percentage of people currently rely on the fallback behaviour of `auth login`.\n\nHowever, in the absence of this, my gut feeling is that it may still be a breaking change worth making, with a clear path to fixing users who are broken (`--insecure-storage` and possibly a config option), particularly in the interactive case.\n\n### What Options are on the Table?\n\n#### Non-Interactivity\n\nI don't think it's acceptable to break scripts out there that are calling `gh auth login --with-token` on environments that don't have a keyring available. However, we can consider some mitigations and nudges:\n * We could add a `--no-insecure-fallback` option for users who really, really don't want this to happen\n * We could add a `no_insecure_fallback` config option for `gh config` that makes it persistent\n * We could add a warning on stderr, just in case someone looks at their script output and realises that something unexpected is happening\n\n#### Interactivity\n\nWith the interactive flows we have a bit more flexibility around changing behaviour. I think there are three paths we could take here, each with a different tradeoff of safety vs convenience.\n\n**`--no-insecure-fallback/no_insecure_fallback` idea from above**\n\nAs above, we could allow users to opt-in to turning this off. The downside of course is that this is opt-in, which even with the warning, still ends up with your token potentially being written to disk.\n\n**Re-prompt**\n\nOn a failure to use the keyring we could offer a prompt along the lines of:\n\n```\nFailed to save the token securely due to <err>\n\nWould you like to continue with insecure storage (y/N) ?\n```\n\nThis is informative and allows for immediate recovery, which is nice since the user has probably gone through a whole oauth dance already.\n\n**Error**\n\nOn a failure to use the keyring we could error out with the reason.\n\nThis might be immediately annoying to a user, but it's a pretty big nudge that they need to start using `--insecure-storage` from now on, which **should** be high friction.\n\n---\n\n### My Recommendation\n\nI think I would probably favour all the non-interactive options, and the last of the interactive options (erroring). I don't love that it's not \"secure by default\" across the board but I really can't see any way in which we can accept breaking the non-interactive usage, which could be [large](https://github.com/search?type=code&q=%22auth+login+--with-token%22).\n\nBoth the re-prompt and error options would also help provide clarity to why keyring operations are failing, since the error is currently suppressed.","reactions":{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/cli/cli/issues/10108/reactions","total_count":5,"+1":5,"-1":0,"laugh":0,"hooray":0,"confused":0,"heart":0,"rocket":0,"eyes":0},"timeline_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/cli/cli/issues/10108/timeline","performed_via_github_app":null,"state_reason":null,"score":1.0},{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/cli/cli/issues/10103","repository_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/cli/cli","labels_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/cli/cli/issues/10103/labels{/name}","comments_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/cli/cli/issues/10103/comments","events_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/cli/cli/issues/10103/events","html_url":"https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/10103","id":2750771557,"node_id":"I_kwDODKw3uc6j9XFl","number":10103,"title":"`remoteResolver` Describe the bug\r\n\r\nWhile writing a GitHub workflow that forks a repo I found that `gh repo fork` only outputs success text if the fork previously existed, not when the fork is newly created. **This issue only appears if running the command from a non-TTY terminal.**\r\n\r\n<details>\r\n<summary>GitHub Workflow Step</summary>\r\n\r\nWhat I currently have to do to get the forked repo name:\r\n\r\n```yaml\r\n- name: Create Fork\r\n  # no-op if the repository is already forked\r\n  shell: bash\r\n  # since this runs from a non-TTY shell, `gh repo fork` doesn't output anything if the\r\n  # fork is newly created, but outputs \"owner/repo already exists\" for existing forks\r\n  run: |\r\n    FORK=$(gh repo fork --clone=false --remote=false --default-branch-only 2>&1)\r\n    if [ -z \"${FORK}\" ]; then\r\n      sleep 60  # it takes a minute before a newly created repo is considered \"existing\"\r\n      FORK=$(gh repo fork --clone=false --remote=false --default-branch-only 2>&1)\r\n    fi\r\n    echo FORK=$(echo \"${FORK}\" | grep -o '[^ ]*/[^ ]*' | head -n1 \\) >> $GITHUB_ENV\r\n  env:\r\n    GH_TOKEN: ${{ inputs.fork-token }}\r\n```\r\n\r\nWhat I'd like to do instead:\r\n\r\n```yaml\r\n- name: Create Fork\r\n  # no-op if the repository is already forked\r\n  shell: bash\r\n  run: echo FORK=$(\r\n    gh repo fork --clone=false --remote=false Describe the bug\n\nWhen there is a PR from a fork in the same org as the upstream, `pr view` will fail to find it, when determining the PR via the branch.\n\nTestscript to demonstrate the issue. Note that we have to use `gh api` to create the PR because `pr create` cannot handle fork in the same org as the upstream for different reasons.\n\n```\n# Setup environment variables used for testscript\nenv REPO=${SCRIPT_NAME}-${RANDOM_STRING}\nenv FORK=${REPO}-fork\n\n# Use gh as a credential helper\nexec gh auth setup-git\n\n# Create a repository to act as upstream with a file so it has a default branch\nexec gh repo create ${ORG}/${REPO} --add-readme --private\n\n# Defer repo cleanup of upstream\ndefer gh repo delete --yes ${ORG}/${REPO}\nexec gh repo view ${ORG}/${REPO} --json id --jq '.id'\nstdout2env REPO_ID\n\n# Create a fork in the same org\nexec gh repo fork ${ORG}/${REPO} --org ${ORG} --fork-name ${FORK}\n\n# Defer repo cleanup of fork\ndefer gh repo delete --yes ${ORG}/${FORK}\nsleep 1\nexec gh repo view ${ORG}/${FORK} --json id --jq '.id'\nstdout2env FORK_ID\n\n# Clone the fork\nexec gh repo clone ${ORG}/${FORK}\ncd ${FORK}\n\n# Prepare a branch\nexec git checkout -b feature-branch\nexec git commit --allow-empty -m 'Empty Commit'\nexec git push -u origin feature-branch\n\n# Create the PR spanning upstream and fork repositories, gh pr create does not support headRepositoryId needed for private forks\nexec gh api graphql -F repositoryId=\"${REPO_ID}\" -F headRepositoryId=\"${FORK_ID}\" -F query='mutation CreatePullRequest($headRepositoryId: ID!, $repositoryId: ID!) { createPullRequest(input:{ baseRefName: \"main\", body: \"Feature Body\", draft: false, headRefName: \"feature-branch\", headRepositoryId: $headRepositoryId, repositoryId: $repositoryId, title:\"Feature Title\" }){ pullRequest{ id url } } }'\n\n# View the PR\nexec gh pr view\nstdout 'Feature Title'\n```\n\n### Implementation Details\n\nWhen using the PR Finder, `gh` will resolve the current branch name from `git`. It then uses the git remote for that branch, and determines whether it should be prepended with `<owner>:`\n\nhttps://github.com/cli/cli/blob/c35d725b0b89ef090aab17171d091f4630eed5ca/pkg/cmd/pr/shared/finder.go#L268-L270\n\nLater, it does another string concatenation based on whether a fetched PR `isCrossRepository`, inside the `pr.HeadLabel()` method, which is then compared to the aforementioned branch:\n\nhttps://github.com/cli/cli/blob/c35d725b0b89ef090aab17171d091f4630eed5ca/pkg/cmd/pr/shared/finder.go#L358\n\nHowever, the original decision to prepend with owner only makes sense if the fork is in a different org.\n\n### Acceptance Criteria\n\n**Given** I have a PR between a fork and upstream in the same org\n**And Given** I have the fork PR branch checked out locally\n**When** I run `gh pr view`\n**Then** I should see the Describe the bug\r\n\r\n`wget -qO- ... | sudo tee` causes any errors from `wget` to be swallowed up, causing an empty keyring file to be configured.\r\n\r\nFor us, the local Ubuntu setup did not have `ca-certificates` installed, which caused `wget` to error out because of a authentication errors (untrusted certificate). However, the `-q` flag and the pipe into `tee` prevented the error from showing up or halting the script.\r\n\r\n### Steps to reproduce the behavior\r\n\r\n1. On an Ubuntu system with no `ca-certificates` installed run the [apt setup script](https://github.com/cli/cli/blob/v2.63.2/docs/install_linux.md#debian-ubuntu-linux-raspberry-pi-os-apt). \r\n(__EDIT__: I ran thiss on a fresh installation of 22.04 with updated packages, so I guess the default system's CA certificates should be somewhat older to reproduce) \r\n2. The output will list errors about a missing public key (`The following signatures couldn't be verified because the public key is not available: NO_PUBKEY 23F3D4EA75716059`), but not about `wget` failing.\r\n\r\n### Expected vs actual behavior\r\n\r\nI expected to see the underlying error from `wget`. I only saw errors about the missing public key.\r\n\r\n### Logs\r\n\r\n```\r\n$ wget https://cli.github.com/packages/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg | sudo tee Describe the bug\r\n\r\nhttps://github.com/cli/cli/commit/8da27d2c8ac8b781cf34a5e04ed57cfe4b68fa55 introduced a path traversal check for artifact downloads in v2.63.1.\r\n\r\nThis traversal check appears to be hitting a false positive with `.thing.output.json`\r\n\r\nSingle file upload, followed by attempt to download fails with path traversal error. Not sure of the cause, perhaps two dots in the filename without being together, or the filename beginning with a dot?\r\n\r\nExpected fails:\r\n`../foo`\r\n`./../foo`\r\n`foo/../bar`\r\n`foo/bar/..`\r\n\r\nExpected successes:\r\n`foo..bar`\r\n`foo.bar.baz`\r\n`.foo`\r\n`foo/.bar`\r\n`.foo/.bar`\r\n\r\n### Steps to reproduce the behavior\r\n\r\nUsing act to emulate GH Actions.\r\n\r\n**NOTE**:\r\n\r\n**Artifact name**: `thing.output.json`\r\n**File name in the artifact**: `.thing.output.json`\r\n\r\n```yaml\r\n      - name: 'Upload Outputs'\r\n        uses: 'actions/upload-artifact@v4'\r\n        with:\r\n          name: 'thing.output.json'\r\n          path: 'path/to/.thing.output.json'\r\n```\r\n\r\n```yaml\r\n      - thing.output.json: would result in path traversal\r\n```\r\n\r\nWorkaround:\r\n\r\nPass the -D option to specify a path to download to:\r\n\r\n```yaml\r\n      - name: 'Download'\r\n        shell: 'bash'\r\n        env:\r\n          GH_TOKEN: '${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}'\r\n        run: |\r\n          set -x;\r\n          mkdir foo;\r\n          ~/.local/opt/gh-v2.63.1/bin/gh run download -n thing.output.json --repo my/repo -D foo;\r\n```\r\n\r\nNo longer errors.\r\n\r\n### Expected vs actual behavior\r\n\r\nExpected: A single file called `.thing.output.json` in an artifact should Describe the bug\n\nA feature I had no idea about is that you can checkout PRs from repositories that are not in your list of git remotes e.g.\n\n```\ngh pr checkout <PR_URL_ON_A_FORK>\n```\n\nWith recent [changes](https://github.com/cli/cli/blob/5a74934d192ddce979ca64377be6e10af8680467/pkg/cmd/pr/checkout/checkout.go#L136) that went in to tighten up credential patterns used for git credential helpers, there is a panic when there is no remote matching the base repo used for the PR.\n\nThis was really a bit of an oversight because there is an obvious case when the `baseRemote` is `nil`: https://github.com/cli/cli/blob/5a74934d192ddce979ca64377be6e10af8680467/pkg/cmd/pr/checkout/checkout.go#L93-L97\n\nThere are probably other [cases](https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/9988) where this is an issue too, but I don't have a clear mental model of them yet. I suspect this probably fixes most of them though.\n\n### Steps to reproduce the behavior\n\nThe following Acceptance Test demonstrates this behaviour:\n\n```\n# Set up env vars\nenv REPO=${SCRIPT_NAME}-${RANDOM_STRING}\n\n# Use gh as a credential helper\nexec gh auth setup-git\n\n# Create a repository with a file so it has a default branch\nexec gh repo create ${ORG}/${REPO} --add-readme --private\n\n# Defer upstream cleanup\ndefer gh repo delete --yes ${ORG}/${REPO}\n\n# Create a fork\nexec gh repo fork ${ORG}/${REPO} --org ${ORG} --fork-name ${REPO}-fork\n\n# Defer fork cleanup\ndefer gh repo delete --yes ${ORG}/${REPO}-fork\n\n# Clone both repos\nexec gh repo clone ${ORG}/${REPO}\nexec gh repo clone ${ORG}/${REPO}-fork\n\n# Prepare a branch to PR in the fork itself\ncd ${REPO}-fork\nexec git checkout -b feature-branch\nexec git commit --allow-empty -m 'Empty Commit'\nexec git push -u origin feature-branch\n\n# Create the PR inside the fork\nexec gh repo set-default ${ORG}/${REPO}-fork\nexec gh pr create --title 'Feature Title' --body 'Feature Body'\nstdout2env PR_URL\n\n# Checkout the PR by full URL in the upstream repo\ncd ${WORK}/${REPO}\nexec gh pr checkout ${PR_URL}\nstderr 'Switched to branch ''feature-branch'''\n```\n\nAnd fails on the checkout with:\n\n```\n...\n > exec gh pr checkout ${PR_URL}\n        [stderr]\n        panic: runtime Describe the bug\n\nWhen there is a PR from a fork in the same org as the upstream, `pr view` will fail to find it, when determining the PR via the branch.\n\nTestscript to demonstrate the issue. Note that we have to use `gh api` to create the PR because `pr create` cannot handle fork in the same org as the upstream for different reasons.\n\n```\n# Setup environment variables used for testscript\nenv REPO=${SCRIPT_NAME}-${RANDOM_STRING}\nenv FORK=${REPO}-fork\n\n# Use gh as a credential helper\nexec gh auth setup-git\n\n# Create a repository to act as upstream with a file so it has a default branch\nexec gh repo create ${ORG}/${REPO} --add-readme --private\n\n# Defer repo cleanup of upstream\ndefer gh repo delete --yes ${ORG}/${REPO}\nexec gh repo view ${ORG}/${REPO} --json id --jq '.id'\nstdout2env REPO_ID\n\n# Create a fork in the same org\nexec gh repo fork ${ORG}/${REPO} --org ${ORG} --fork-name ${FORK}\n\n# Defer repo cleanup of fork\ndefer gh repo delete --yes ${ORG}/${FORK}\nsleep 1\nexec gh repo view ${ORG}/${FORK} --json id --jq '.id'\nstdout2env FORK_ID\n\n# Clone the fork\nexec gh repo clone ${ORG}/${FORK}\ncd ${FORK}\n\n# Prepare a branch\nexec git checkout -b feature-branch\nexec git commit --allow-empty -m 'Empty Commit'\nexec git push -u origin feature-branch\n\n# Create the PR spanning upstream and fork repositories, gh pr create does not support headRepositoryId needed for private forks\nexec gh api graphql -F repositoryId=\"${REPO_ID}\" -F headRepositoryId=\"${FORK_ID}\" -F query='mutation CreatePullRequest($headRepositoryId: ID!, $repositoryId: ID!) { createPullRequest(input:{ baseRefName: \"main\", body: \"Feature Body\", draft: false, headRefName: \"feature-branch\", headRepositoryId: $headRepositoryId, repositoryId: $repositoryId, title:\"Feature Title\" }){ pullRequest{ id url } } }'\n\n# View the PR\nexec gh pr view\nstdout 'Feature Title'\n```\n\n### Implementation Details\n\nWhen using the PR Finder, `gh` will resolve the current branch name from `git`. It then uses the git remote for that branch, and determines whether it should be prepended with `<owner>:`\n\nhttps://github.com/cli/cli/blob/c35d725b0b89ef090aab17171d091f4630eed5ca/pkg/cmd/pr/shared/finder.go#L268-L270\n\nLater, it does another string concatenation based on whether a fetched PR `isCrossRepository`, inside the `pr.HeadLabel()` method, which is then compared to the aforementioned branch:\n\nhttps://github.com/cli/cli/blob/c35d725b0b89ef090aab17171d091f4630eed5ca/pkg/cmd/pr/shared/finder.go#L358\n\nHowever, the original decision to prepend with owner only makes sense if the fork is in a different org.\n\n### Acceptance Criteria\n\n**Given** I have a PR between a fork and upstream in the same org\n**And Given** I have the fork PR branch checked out locally\n**When** I run `gh pr view`\n**Then** I should see the Run GitHub CLI command below to verify attestations offline\r\n\r\n#### GH_DEBUG=false\r\n```\r\ngh attestation verify PATH/TO/YOUR/BUILD/ARTIFACT-BINARY -R ORGANIZATION_NAME/REPOSITORY_NAME --bundle attestation.jsonl --custom-trusted-root trusted_root.jsonl\r\nLoaded digest sha256:XYZ for file://ARTIFACT-BINARY\r\nLoaded 16 attestations from attestation.jsonl\r\n✗ Verification failed\r\n\r\nError: verifying with issuer \"GitHub, Inc.\"\r\n```\r\n\r\n#### GH_DEBUG=true\r\n```\r\nGH_DEBUG=true gh attestation verify PATH/TO/YOUR/BUILD/ARTIFACT-BINARY -R ORGANIZATION_NAME/REPOSITORY_NAME --bundle attestation.jsonl --custom-trusted-root trusted_root.jsonl\r\nLoaded digest sha256:XYZ for file://ARTIFACT-BINARY\r\nLoaded 16 attestations from attestation.jsonl\r\nVerifying attestation 1/16 against the configured Sigstore trust roots\r\nAttempting verification against issuer \"GitHub, Inc.\"\r\nSUCCESS - attestation signature verified with \"GitHub, Inc.\"\r\n\r\nVerifying attestation 2/16 against the configured Sigstore trust roots\r\nAttempting verification against issuer \"GitHub, Inc.\"\r\nFailed to verify against issuer \"GitHub, Inc.\"\r\n\r\n✗ Verification failed\r\n\r\nError: verifying with issuer \"GitHub, Inc.\"\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Expected vs actual behavior\r\n\r\n#### Expected\r\nWhen running the command with `GH_DEBUG=true` you can see in the output that the actual verification was successful for the `<file-path>` parameter that was passed by the command, i.e. -> `PATH/TO/YOUR/BUILD/ARTIFACT-BINARY`. However the verification fails as the GitHub CLI is looping the bundle files and trying to validate all of them when only a single files is specified on the command. This would make sense if the GitHub CLI allowed a glob pattern to be passed to verify multiple binary artifacts given the bundle but I do not think this is supported.\r\n\r\n#### Actual\r\nVerification fails with error `Error: verifying with issuer \"GitHub, Inc.\"`\r\n\r\n#### Observations\r\nIt seems the CLI command `gh at verify` should support a glob pattern to verify multiple files when using the `--bundle` parameter or only try to verify the single file that was passed in the `<file-path>` Describe the bug\r\n\r\nhttps://github.com/cli/cli/commit/8da27d2c8ac8b781cf34a5e04ed57cfe4b68fa55 introduced a path traversal check for artifact downloads in v2.63.1.\r\n\r\nThis traversal check appears to be hitting a false positive with `.thing.output.json`\r\n\r\nSingle file upload, followed by attempt to download fails with path traversal error. Not sure of the cause, perhaps two dots in the filename without being together, or the filename beginning with a dot?\r\n\r\nExpected fails:\r\n`../foo`\r\n`./../foo`\r\n`foo/../bar`\r\n`foo/bar/..`\r\n\r\nExpected successes:\r\n`foo..bar`\r\n`foo.bar.baz`\r\n`.foo`\r\n`foo/.bar`\r\n`.foo/.bar`\r\n\r\n### Steps to reproduce the behavior\r\n\r\nUsing act to emulate GH Actions.\r\n\r\n**NOTE**:\r\n\r\n**Artifact name**: `thing.output.json`\r\n**File name in the artifact**: `.thing.output.json`\r\n\r\n```yaml\r\n      - name: 'Upload Outputs'\r\n        uses: 'actions/upload-artifact@v4'\r\n        with:\r\n          name: 'thing.output.json'\r\n          path: 'path/to/.thing.output.json'\r\n```\r\n\r\n```yaml\r\n      - thing.output.json: would result in path traversal\r\n```\r\n\r\nWorkaround:\r\n\r\nPass the -D option to specify a path to download to:\r\n\r\n```yaml\r\n      - name: 'Download'\r\n        shell: 'bash'\r\n        env:\r\n          GH_TOKEN: '${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}'\r\n        run: |\r\n          set -x;\r\n          mkdir foo;\r\n          ~/.local/opt/gh-v2.63.1/bin/gh run download -n thing.output.json --repo my/repo -D foo;\r\n```\r\n\r\nNo longer errors.\r\n\r\n### Expected vs actual behavior\r\n\r\nExpected: A single file called `.thing.output.json` in an artifact should Describe the bug\n\nA feature I had no idea about is that you can checkout PRs from repositories that are not in your list of git remotes e.g.\n\n```\ngh pr checkout <PR_URL_ON_A_FORK>\n```\n\nWith recent [changes](https://github.com/cli/cli/blob/5a74934d192ddce979ca64377be6e10af8680467/pkg/cmd/pr/checkout/checkout.go#L136) that went in to tighten up credential patterns used for git credential helpers, there is a panic when there is no remote matching the base repo used for the PR.\n\nThis was really a bit of an oversight because there is an obvious case when the `baseRemote` is `nil`: https://github.com/cli/cli/blob/5a74934d192ddce979ca64377be6e10af8680467/pkg/cmd/pr/checkout/checkout.go#L93-L97\n\nThere are probably other [cases](https://github.com/cli/cli/issues/9988) where this is an issue too, but I don't have a clear mental model of them yet. I suspect this probably fixes most of them though.\n\n### Steps to reproduce the behavior\n\nThe following Acceptance Test demonstrates this behaviour:\n\n```\n# Set up env vars\nenv REPO=${SCRIPT_NAME}-${RANDOM_STRING}\n\n# Use gh as a credential helper\nexec gh auth setup-git\n\n# Create a repository with a file so it has a default branch\nexec gh repo create ${ORG}/${REPO} --add-readme --private\n\n# Defer upstream cleanup\ndefer gh repo delete --yes ${ORG}/${REPO}\n\n# Create a fork\nexec gh repo fork ${ORG}/${REPO} --org ${ORG} --fork-name ${REPO}-fork\n\n# Defer fork cleanup\ndefer gh repo delete --yes ${ORG}/${REPO}-fork\n\n# Clone both repos\nexec gh repo clone ${ORG}/${REPO}\nexec gh repo clone ${ORG}/${REPO}-fork\n\n# Prepare a branch to PR in the fork itself\ncd ${REPO}-fork\nexec git checkout -b feature-branch\nexec git commit --allow-empty -m 'Empty Commit'\nexec git push -u origin feature-branch\n\n# Create the PR inside the fork\nexec gh repo set-default ${ORG}/${REPO}-fork\nexec gh pr create --title 'Feature Title' --body 'Feature Body'\nstdout2env PR_URL\n\n# Checkout the PR by full URL in the upstream repo\ncd ${WORK}/${REPO}\nexec gh pr checkout ${PR_URL}\nstderr 'Switched to branch ''feature-branch'''\n```\n\nAnd fails on the checkout with:\n\n```\n...\n > exec gh pr checkout ${PR_URL}\n        [stderr]\n        panic: runtime Describe the bug\n\nWhen running `gh repo fork` in the context of an existing repo, the CLI offers to create a remote for the fork.:\n\n```\n? Would you like to add a remote for the fork? Clone a repo from GitHub with `gh repo clone` and `cd` into it. The new repo will have an `origin` remote configured.\n\n```bash\ngh remote clone timrogers/litra-rs && cd litra-rs\n```\n\n2. Describe the bug\r\n\r\nOn repos with merge queues enabled, using `gh pr merge` with `--delete-branch` forces the PR to be closed before merging it.\r\n\r\nA clear and concise description of what the bug is.\r\n\r\n`gh --version`:  `2.23.0`\r\n\r\n### Steps to reproduce the behavior\r\n\r\n1. Create a repo in a team / enterprise org.\r\n2. Enable merge queues.\r\n3. Create a PR.\r\n4. On the branch for the PR, using `gh`, run:  `gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch`\r\n5. Output in terminal:\r\n```text\r\n! The merge strategy for main is set by the merge queue\r\n✓ Pull request #1 will be added to the merge queue for main when ready\r\nFrom https://github.com/org/repo\r\n * branch              main       -> FETCH_HEAD\r\nAlready up to date.\r\n✓ Deleted branch fix/merge-queue-issue and switched to branch main\r\n```\r\n6. On the repo, PR gets added to the merge queue.\r\n7. But, the remote branch is deleted immediately after (before merge), which closes the PR and removes it from the merge queue.\r\n\r\n### Expected vs actual behavior\r\n\r\nAfter 5:\r\n6. On the repo, PR gets added to the merge queue.\r\n7. Remote branch is persisted until merge.\r\n8. Merge queue is executed nominally.\r\n9. If merge is successful, delete remote branch, mark PR as merged. If merge fails, keep remote branch and keep PR
